Sonnet 4-6 leads with 99% field match accuracy but takes 34% longer than GPT-4 Turbo

Five frontier models tested across 350 extraction runs show consistent quality gains, with OpenAI's latest closing the gap on Claude.

BEST OVERALL QUALITY
Sonnet 4-6: 98.9%

350 runs100.00% success2.71 mismatch

Claude Sonnet 4-6 achieved 98.9% field match accuracy with an average of just 0.29 mismatches per run—dramatically outperforming all competitors. Opus 4-6 took second place at 92.3%, while Gemini 2.5 Pro lagged at 83.3%.
SPEED VS QUALITY TRADEOFF
10.6 sec vs 14.2 sec

0.29 mismatch14.2s avg0 regressions

OpenAI's GPT-4 Turbo (5.4) delivered 88% field match in just 10.6 seconds—34% faster than Sonnet 4-6's 14.2 seconds. For latency-sensitive pipelines, GPT-4 Turbo offers a compelling balance of speed and accuracy, though it still trails Claude by 11 percentage points.
CROSS-DATASET CONSISTENCY
88–91% range

4.40 mismatch44.0s avg18 regressions

Field match rates stayed remarkably tight across all three datasets: 90.6% on acme_foods, 88.3% on downloaded, and 90.2% on nova_exports. This narrow 2.3-point spread suggests the extraction pipeline generalizes well, with no single dataset proving uniquely difficult.

Sec. 03

Quality vs. speed

The chart below plots each model's field match rate against average runtime. Sonnet 4-6 sits at the top-right (highest quality, moderate speed), while GPT-4 Turbo clusters near the bottom-left (faster but less accurate). Gemini 2.5 Pro is the outlier: slowest runtime (44 seconds) with the lowest accuracy (83.3%).

Sec. 05

Few-shot sweep

All models ran in zero-shot mode (fs_count = 0), meaning no few-shot examples were provided. This isolates each model's baseline instruction-following ability without prompt engineering shortcuts.

Sec. 06

What to check next

Based on these results, prioritize the following steps to optimize your extraction pipeline and address quality gaps.

01
Deploy Sonnet 4-6 for production accuracy
With 98.9% field match and only 0.29 mismatches per run, Sonnet 4-6 is the clear choice for high-stakes extraction where quality trumps speed. Budget for the 14-second average latency.
02
Prototype with GPT-4 Turbo for speed-sensitive workflows
If sub-11-second response times are critical (e.g., real-time dashboards), GPT-4 Turbo's 88% accuracy may be acceptable. Run side-by-side tests on your highest-value documents to quantify the quality-speed tradeoff.
03
Investigate Gemini 2.5 Pro's poor performance
Gemini 2.5 Pro's 83.3% accuracy and 44-second runtime make it non-competitive here. If you're locked into Google infrastructure, experiment with few-shot prompts or consider Gemini 2.0 Flash as a faster alternative.
04
Add few-shot examples to lift middle-tier models
OpenAI 5.2 and Opus 4-6 sit at 84.6% and 92.3% respectively. Providing 2–3 annotated examples per dataset could narrow the gap to Sonnet 4-6 without retraining.
05
Monitor regression cases across all datasets
The brief flags 7 to 12 regressions per dataset where post-processing worsened accuracy. Audit these outliers to ensure your validation rules aren't over-correcting correct LLM outputs.
06
Benchmark token costs alongside runtime
Total token usage ranged from 782k to 1.1M tokens per dataset. With Sonnet 4-6 and Opus 4-6 on volume pricing, calculate $/run to confirm whether the quality premium justifies the cost at scale.

How to read these numbers

SUCCESS RATE — Share of runs that finished without a harness or HTTP error. High success means the run was stable; it does not prove the answers matched the reference.

AVG ELAPSED (S) — Average wall time per run in that bucket. Useful for latency comparisons.

AVG MISMATCH / EXPECTED RUN — Average count of fields that differed from the golden JSON when a reference existed. Lower is better.

FIELD MATCH — Fraction of compared fields that matched the golden output across runs in that bucket. Higher is better.
